    dot**, same devices (the o printed pink).
  - p.81 Ex.2 Complete the sentence with the correct word from the
    box (pot – dog – mop – hop – log): "I can see a ___." (pot) /
    "I see a ___." (mop) / "There is a ___." (log) / "I can see a
    ___." (dog) / "The boy can ___." (hop) — the family words now
    finish real sentences, the picture beside each as the guide.
  - p.82 Ex.3 Choose the correct word (pot: dog/pot; fog: fog/hot;
    log: mop/log; pop: hop/pop; dot: fog/dot; hot: hot/pot).
  - p.83 Ex.4 **Chant with rhyming words** — one dog's whole story:
    "I saw a silly dog! / It got lost in the fog! / It tripped over
    a log! / It started to hop, hop, hop! / Then heard a balloon go
    pop! / It found a cooking pot! / The pot was very hot!" · Ex.5
    Listen to the chant and answer using the words below (word box:
    hop – fog – pot – dog – hot – pop – log; the chant's seven lines
    re-printed with blanks, in a shuffled order — the learner
    rebuilds the story from memory of its sounds).
  - p.84 Writing Practice (Ex.6): nine lined boxes headed dog, fog,
    log / pot, hot, dot / mop, hop, pop — the learner fills each box
    writing that word.

### P1T2 Unit 5 "Neighborhood" — page by page (PDF pp.85–101; read 2026-10-25)
- p.85 Unit opener + Learning Outcomes: name familiar places; ask
  and answer simple questions about places; recognize common action
  words; recognize the short vowel sound /ʌ/ in familiar words;
  recognize word families; identify rhyming words; read CVC words
  with short vowel sound /ʌ/; identify and pronounce consonant
  blends with letter (l) in familiar words; recognize sight words.
- **Lesson 1: Around My Town** (pp.86–89)
  - p.86 Ex.1 Listen and repeat — eight town words on pictures:
    **hospital, school, shop, building, street, car, truck, bus**
    (the hospital is shown with its ambulance; the school is a
    grand columned building).
  - p.87 Dialog (Listen): Sara: "Let's walk around town, Ali!" ·
    Ali: "Okay! Look, this is a school." · Sara: "And where do you
    go when you feel sick?" · Ali: "I go to the hospital." · Sara:
    "What do you see on the street?" · Ali: "I see a truck, a bus,
    and a car." · Sara: "Wow! And what's that big building over
    there?" · Ali: "That's a shop. We can buy things there." ·
    **Sight words: is – a – do – you – go – I – to – see – on – the
    – and** · Ex.3 Listen and circle (hospital, shop, street, bus —
    each word over two pictures).
  - p.88 Ex.4 Check (✓) if the word matches the picture, (✗) if it
    doesn't — the first one is worked: "hospital" under a bus is
    marked ✗ in red (street ✓, school ✓, shop under "truck" ✗, the
    hospital under "bus" ✗, building ✓) · Ex.5 Match the word to the
    correct picture (hospital, shop, street, bus, truck, school).
  - p.89 Ex.6 Check (✓) the correct picture (shop, building, street,
    car — each name over two pictures).
- **Lesson 2: Phonics — Short Vowel /ʌ/, Word Families** (pp.90–93)
  - p.90 Ex.1 Listen, read and repeat: **"-ug" family — hug, jug,
    mug · "-un" family — bun, run, sun · "-ut" family — hut, nut,
    cut**, same devices as before (dots, blending arrow, pink u).
    With this lesson the book has given word families for **all
    five short vowels**: /æ/ and /ɛ/ (Unit 3), /ɪ/ and /ɒ/ (Unit 4),
    /ʌ/ (Unit 5).
  - p.91 Ex.2 Choose the correct word — now from **three** printed
    words per picture (jug: jug/mug/hug; sun: bun/sun/run; bun:
    bun/sun/run; hut: cut/nut/hut; mug: jug/mug/hug; nut: cut/nut/
    hut).
  - p.92 Ex.3 **Word search** — the book's first: "Look for the
    hidden words using the list of words given" — a 9×9 letter grid
    holding the nine short-u words (jug is found and circled, and
    crossed off the "Short u" list: jug, hug, mug, sun, bun, run,
    hut, cut, nut) · Ex.4 Write using words from the box (hug – run
    – cut; three action pictures with empty labels).
  - p.93 Ex.5 **Chant with rhyming words**: "I found a nut near my
    hut, / I used a knife to cut. // I held my mug, I filled it up /
    with milk from the jug. // I ate a bun and went for a run, I /
    looked at the bright sun." · Ex.6 Find the words in the chant
    and write ("A word rhyming with sun (___) / with hut (___) /
    with hug (___)") · Ex.7 Match the rhyming words (bun, nut, jug
    against hut, mug, sun — word + its own picture on each side).
- **Lesson 3: Places I Visit** (pp.94–97)
  - p.94 Ex.1 Listen and repeat — three places, each with what you
    do there: **Park** — jump ("I can jump."), throw ("I can throw
    the ball."), catch ("I can catch the ball.") · **Zoo** —
    animals ("I see animals in the zoo.") · **Beach** — dig, swim,
    run (words only, no sentences).
  - p.95 Dialog (Listen): Ali: "Good morning, Sara. What are your
    plans for today?" · Sara: "Good morning, Ali. Let's go to the
    park." · Ali: "Great idea. I can run and throw the ball." ·
    Sara: "And I will catch it!" · Ali: "Tomorrow, let's visit the
    zoo." · Sara: "Yes. We can see so many animals there." · Ali:
    "And next week, let's go to the beach." · Sara: "Awesome! We can
    swim, play in the sand, and dig together." · Ali: "That will be
    so much fun." · **Sight words: go – to – the – I – can – will –
    it – we – see – there – and – so – be – in – for – are** · Ex.3
    "Guess where we are…" — the book's first little definitions to
    solve: "The place where we can see animals. It is the (beach –
    zoo)." / "The place where we can swim and dig. It is the (park –
    beach)." / "The place where we can throw and catch the ball. It
    is the (park – zoo)."
  - p.96 Ex.4 Circle the word that matches the picture, inside "I
    can …" sentences ("I can (swim – jump)." / "I can (throw –
    dig)." / "I can (swim – catch)." / "I can (run – throw).") ·
    Ex.5 Check (✓)/(✗) word–picture matches (catch ✓, beach ✓, run
    ✓, zoo under a street ✗).
  - p.97 Ex.6 Write the number next to the correct picture (1 park,
    2 zoo, 3 animals, 4 beach, 5 run, 6 dig — six words, six
    pictures).
- **Lesson 4: Phonics — Consonant Blends with (l)** (pp.98–100)
  - p.98 "Consonant Blends — Blend with letter (l)!" · Ex.1 Listen
    to each sound, blend, and repeat: **cl → clock · bl → black ·
    fl → flag · sl → slide** — same arrow-box device as the s-blends;
    the flag pictured is **the flag of Egypt**.
  - p.99 Ex.2 Match the picture with the correct consonant blend
    (black, slide, clock, flag → cl, bl, fl, sl) · Ex.3 Choose the
    correct consonant blend (clock: fl/cl; slide: gl/Sl; flag:
    fl/bl; black: bl/cl — gl appears as a wrong choice, never
    taught).
  - p.100 "More Blend Words!" (Ex.5 — the lesson's numbering skips
    4): "Can you think of more words that start with the same
    blends: cl, bl, fl, and sl?" — four boxes give only the blend
    and a hint picture (a cloud for cl, the black splat for bl, a
    flower for fl, a sleeping boy for sl); no word is printed — the
    learner must think of the word the picture points to.
- p.101 Writing Practice (Ex.6): nine lined boxes headed hug, jug,
  mug / bun, run, sun / hut, nut, cut — the learner fills each box
  writing that word (the /ʌ/ words of Lesson 2).

Unit 5 (Term 2) basics filed: the town set (hospital, school, shop,
building, street, car, truck, bus) with the "Where do you go when you
feel sick? I go to the hospital." and "What do you see on the street?
I see a …" patterns, the visit places (park, zoo, beach) with their
action words (jump, throw, catch, run, swim, dig) and "I can …"
sentences, and the unit's sight words are recorded here for the Word
and Sentence titles in ladder order. The /ʌ/ families (-ug, -un, -ut)
— completing all five short vowels — and the (l) blends (cl, bl, fl,
sl) are filed in the book map above and noted in the-alphabet
sources — Word-step material.

### P1T2 Unit 6 "Story: Two Friends and One Apple" + Review 2 + Glossary + back cover — page by page (PDF pp.102–112; read 2026-10-25)
- p.102 Unit opener + Learning Outcomes: identify story elements;
  answer comprehension questions about story elements; discuss the
  feelings of characters at different points of the story; discuss
  the moral of the story; recognize sight words. (Per the Scope and
  Sequence this unit has no new Language or Phonics — it re-uses the
  whole year.)
- **The story itself** (pp.103–104) — the year's first full story,
  told in the past tense, five pictures with text under each:
  - p.103 "Two Friends and One Apple" · "One sunny morning, Omar and
    Sara went to the park. They were running, playing, and having
    fun!" · "Sara found a big, shiny red apple. She held it happily
    in her hands." · "Omar saw Sara holding the apple. He felt a
    little sad because he didn't have one." · **Sight words: a – it
    – in – he – have – and – to – the**.
  - p.104 "Sara noticed Omar's sad face. She wanted to make him
    happy. So, she took the apple and carefully split it into two
    pieces." · "Sara gave one half of the apple to Omar and kept one
    half for herself. Omar said 'Thank you, Sara'. His smile came
    back, and they both enjoyed the apple together. They learned
    that sharing makes everyone happy!" · **Moral (printed in its
    own box): "It's always kind to share with others."** · **Sight
    words: he – to – so – the – and – it – of – for – you – they**.
- p.105 **Story Elements** — the book teaches the parts of a story
  by name, kept here as printed: "1. Characters (Who?) • Omar •
  Sara. 2. Setting (Where & When?) • In the park • One sunny
  morning. 3. Problem (What's wrong?) • Omar felt sad because he
  didn't have an apple. 4. Events (What happens?) • Omar and Sara
  played in the park. • Sara found a big red apple. • Omar felt sad
  when he saw the apple as he didn't have one. • Sara noticed his
  sadness. • Sara split the apple into two pieces. • She gave one
  half to Omar. 5. Solution (How is the problem solved?) • Sara
  shared the apple with Omar. 6. Ending / Lesson (What do we learn?)
  • Omar was happy again. • They enjoyed the apple together. •
  Sharing makes everyone happy!" · Ex.1 Put in Order — number the
  sentences 1–4 ("Sara gave half the apple to Omar. / Sara found a
  red apple. / Omar and Sara went to the park. [given: 1] / Omar
  felt sad.").
- p.106 Ex.2 Listen and circle the correct answer — six questions
  about the story with two printed answers each ("Who are the
  characters in the story? Sara and Ali / Sara and Omar · Where
  does the story happen? in the park / at home · What does Sara
  find? A banana / An apple · How does Omar feel when he sees the
  apple? happy / sad · What does Sara decide to do? eat the apple /
  share the apple · How do the friends feel at the end? happy /
  sad") · Ex.3 Speaking — discuss with your teacher ("Characters:
  What are the names of the characters in the story? Place: Where
  did the story take place? Problem: What was the problem in the
  story? Solution: How did Sara solve the problem? Moral: What
  lesson did you learn?") · Ex.4 Match each picture with the correct
  word (apple, happy face, sad face, Sara, Omar → sad, Sara, apple,
  Omar, happy) · Ex.5 **Role Play: Act out the story with a
  friend.**
- **Review 2** (pp.107–110) — covers Units 4–6:
  - p.107 Ex.1 Read the word, then circle the correct picture (dog,
    bin, lip, hot, fin, jug, mop, log — the learner reads each
    printed family word first, then finds its picture among three;
    several wrong pictures are other family words' pictures).
  - p.108 Ex.2 Check (✓) the correct word from three printed words
    (sun: sun/fun/bun — **fun** appears here, a word never taught,
    used only as a wrong choice; big elephant: dig/big/fig; dig:
    big/fig/dig; hot: pot/dot/hot; fig: fig/big/dig; hut: cut/hut/
    nut; pot: pot/dot/hop; run: fun/bun/run).
  - p.109 Ex.3 Check (✓) the correct blending letters (skip:
    bl/sk; slide: sl/fr; swing: sw/fl; flag: fl/cl; snail: sl/sn;
    black: bl/st) · Ex.4 Choose the correct consonant blend from
    three (snail: fl/sn/st; flag: sw/bl/fl; slide: fl/sw/sl; strong:
    st/sl/sk; clock: cl/sn/bl; black: sn/cl/bl) — all eight blends
    of the year mixed together.
  - p.110 Ex.5 Circle the odd one out (three rows of four pictures:
    rooms with a cap among them; town buildings with a mug among
    them; visit places with a banana among them) · Ex.6 Match the
    word with the picture (jump, throw, run, swim).
- p.111 **Glossary** — one page, the year's words A–Z with the
  book's own definitions, kept word for word: angry "feeling or
  showing anger" · animals "any of a kingdom of living things
  composed of many cells typically differing from plants in capacity
  for active movement" · bathroom "a room containing a bathtub or
  shower and usually a sink and toilet" · beach "a sandy or gravelly
  part of the shore of a body of water" · bed "a piece of furniture
  on which to lie or sleep" · bedroom "a room used for sleeping" ·
  bread "a baked food made of flour" · breakfast "the first meal of
  the day" · brush "to clean, smooth, or scrub with a brush" ·
  building "a permanent structure built as a dwelling, shelter, or
  place for human activity or for storage" · bus "a large motor
  vehicle for carrying passengers" · car "a vehicle moving on
  wheels" · catch "to capture or seize in flight or motion" · chair
  "a seat with legs and a back for use by one person" · chicken "the
  common domestic fowl, its flesh used as food" · closet "a cabinet
  or small room for clothing" · cloudy "covered over by clouds" ·
  cold "having a low temperature or one much below normal" · cook
  "to prepare food for eating especially by the use of heat" · couch
  "a piece of furniture (as a sofa) that one can sit or lie on" ·
  dig "to hollow out or form by removing earth" · dining room "a
  room used for eating meals" · dinner "a light meal eaten at night"
  · drink "to swallow liquid" · eat "to take into the mouth and
  swallow food" · fall "the season between summer and winter that
  usually includes the months of September, October, and November" ·
  fridge "a device or room for keeping articles (as food) cool" ·
  happy "enjoying well-being and contentment" · hospital "an
  institution where the sick or injured are given medical or
  surgical care" · hot "having a high temperature" · juice "the
  liquid part that can be squeezed out of vegetables and fruits" ·
  jump "to spring into the air" · kitchen "a room in which cooking
  is done" · living room "a room in a house for general family use"
  · lunch "the main meal eaten in the middle of the day" · mask
  "something you wear to cover your face" (mask is in the glossary
  only — it appears in no lesson, the same glossary-only pattern as
  Term 1) · oven "a chamber (as in a stove) used for baking,
  heating, or drying" · park "an area maintained in its natural
  state as a public property" · rainy "having much rain" · rice "a
  grass widely grown in warm wet areas especially for its seeds
  which are used for food" · run "to go at a pace faster than a
  walk" · sad "filled with or expressing grief or unhappiness" ·
  sandwich "one or more slices of bread or a roll with a filling or
  spread" · scared "thrown into or being in a state of fear, fright,
  or panic" · school "a place or establishment for teaching and
  learning" · shop "a building or room where goods are sold retail"
  · sink "a stationary basin for washing (as in a kitchen) connected
  with a drain and usually a water supply" · soup "a liquid food
  with stock as a base and often containing pieces of solid food" ·
  spring "the season between winter and summer that usually includes
  the months of March, April, and May" · street "a public way
  especially in a city, town, or village usually including
  sidewalks" · summer "the season between spring and autumn that
  usually includes the months of June, July, and August" · sunny
  "bright with sunshine" · surpised [sic — spelled so in the
  glossary, as on p.25] "feeling or showing surprise because of
  something unexpected" · swim "to move through water by moving arms,
  legs, fins, or tail" · table "a piece of furniture with a smooth
  flat top fixed on legs" · throw "to send through the air
  especially with a quick forward motion of the arm" · tired
  "drained of strength and energy" · toilet "a fixture that consists
  usually of a bowl that is flushed with water and a seat" · truck
  "a wheeled vehicle for moving heavy articles" · TV "a television
  receiving set" · warm "having or giving off heat to a moderate or
  adequate degree" · watch TV "to keep one's eyes on TV" · windy
  "having strong winds" · winter "the season between fall and spring
  that usually includes the months of December, January, and
  February" · zoo "a garden or park where living usually wild
  animals are kept for showing to the public".
- p.112 Back cover: "English — Primary 1 – Term 2 — Academic Year:
  2025/2026" · "No part of this publication may be reproduced…" ·
  Editorial Note (same diverse-team text as Term 1) · Developed and
  produced with the participation of the Content Department Team at

Unit 6 (Term 2) basics filed: the story "Two Friends and One Apple"
is recorded in full above with its two sight-word strips and its
printed moral ("It's always kind to share with others."), and the
book's own Story Elements frame — Characters (Who?), Setting (Where
& When?), Problem (What's wrong?), Events (What happens?), Solution
(How is the problem solved?), Ending / Lesson (What do we learn?) —
is kept word for word; both belong to the Story title when built.
The Term 2 Glossary's definitions are kept word for word above for
the Word title. Review 2 adds no new basics.

**Egypt Primary 1 Term 2 — READ COMPLETE 2026-10-25 (all 112
pages).** Together with Term 1, Egypt Primary 1 English is fully
read: all 26 letters (Term 1's 20 in sounds order + Term 2's Ww Qq
Vv Zz Yy Xx), two blend sets (s-blends, l-blends), word families for
all five short vowels, sight words in every lesson, days, months,
numbers 1–20, and the year's first full story with its story-elements
frame.

Unit 4 (Term 2) basics filed: the rooms set (bedroom, living room,
kitchen, dining room, bathroom) with the "I … in the …" room
sentences, the furniture set (bed, closet, chairs, table, couch, TV,
fridge, oven, sink, toilet) tied room by room with the "It has a …
and a …" and "What can we find in a …? We can find …" patterns, and
the unit's sight words are recorded here for the Word and Sentence
titles in ladder order. The /ɪ/ families (-ig, -in, -ip) and /ɒ/
families (-og, -op, -ot) are filed in the book map above and noted in
the-alphabet sources — Word-step material.

Unit 3 (Term 2) basics filed: the months set with their numbers
(January 1 – December 12), the "When is your birthday? My birthday is
in …" and month before/after patterns, the seasons set (spring,
summer, fall, winter) and weather set (warm, hot, sunny, cold, windy,
rainy, cloudy) with the season sentences ("Spring is warm and
sunny." / "What's the weather like in summer?" / "Is it cold in
winter? Yes, it is."), and the first sight-word strips of the unit
(my, is, in, your · a, and, on, the, put, an · is, it, the, I, we,
can, go, do, you, to) are recorded here for the Word and Sentence
titles in ladder order. The /æ/ families (-an, -ap, -at) and /ɛ/
families (-et, -en) with their blending-arrow device and rhyming
chants are filed in the book map above and noted in the-alphabet
sources as the book's first whole-word reading — they belong to the
Word title when built.

## Built so far
- greetings/hello-and-your-name/ — built from Unit 1, Lesson 1 (see its
  sources.md). Title = the common-sense name (Greetings), not the book's
  unit name — per his title rule, 2026-10-25.

## KG1 Term 1 — read from the rendered pages 2026-10-25

The book starts BEFORE letters, at the true bottom:

- **Book p.1:** "Let's learn how to hold a pencil correctly" — 5 pictures
  of the pencil grip, then trace-the-lines practice (wavy and zigzag
  lines). No letters yet.
- **Contents (PDF p.3, two units on the page):**
  - Unit 1 "Meet and Greet!" — Vocabulary: hello, good morning, goodbye,
    boy, girl, friends, happy, sad · Phonics: **t** (train, toys),
    **T** (turtle), **i** (in, insect), **I** (ink) · Language focus:
    using simple greetings; recognizing the sound of letters Tt and Ii.
  - Unit 2 "I Can Find it at School!" — Vocabulary: classroom, teacher,
    chair, table, board, book, pencil, eraser, playground, swing, slide ·
    Phonics: **s** (star, sun), **S** (school), **a** (apple, ant),
    **n** (nest, night), **N** (numbers).
- Unit 1 pages: "Friends" picture page (book p.3), "Look and Match:
  Good morning / Goodbye" (book p.5) — in KG the greetings are spoken
  and picture-matched, NOT read as sentences; the reading line is the
  letter sounds (Tt, Ii first), exactly the bottom-up order.

So the English path's first builds, in order: (1) Pencil and Lines
(promoted out of English into its own prerequisite course, 2026-10-25);
(2) Letter — the sounds of Tt and Ii (KG1 Unit 1 phonics, repeated in
Primary 1 Unit 1).

### KG1 full contents (PDF pp.3–4, read complete)
- Unit 3 "My Body, My Care!" — body words (hair, head, face, eyes,
  nose, mouth, teeth, arms, hands, legs, feet) · Phonics: **p/P**
  (pen, pants / pan), **h/H** (heart, honey / helicopter), **d/D**
  (dog, desk / dress), **r/R** (rabbit, robot / rice) · Number 3,
  star shape, yellow.
- Unit 4 "My Family Picnic!" — family + food words (grandfather,
  grandmother, father, mother, sister, brother, tree, flower, grass,
  fruits, chocolate, chips, juice, sandwich) · Phonics: **e/E**
  (egg, eagle / elephant), **o** (cow, cloud), **C/c** (cap), **k/K**
  (kiwi, kick / kite), **m/M** (mom, man / mud) · Numbers 4 and 5,
  heart + triangle shapes, green and orange.
- Review (units 1–4): letters **Tt Ii Ss Aa Nn Pp Hh Dd Rr Ee Cc Kk
  Mm** — 13 letters in this order; numbers 1–5; shapes circle,
  square, star, heart, triangle; colors red, blue, yellow, green,
  orange. Project: My Classroom.

### KG1 Unit 1 — page by page (book pp.2–17; read 2026-10-25)
- p.2 Unit opener: two children at school — "Hi." / "Hello."
- p.3 "Friends" (picture page)
- p.4 "Good morning." (arriving at school, morning) / "Goodbye."
  (leaving, sunset) — the day in two greetings
- p.5 Look and Match: Good morning / Goodbye to their pictures
- p.6 "A boy or a girl?" — "I'm a boy." / "I'm a girl." (Word bank:
  Boy-Girl)
- p.7 "How are you feeling today?" — "I'm happy." / "I'm sad."
  (Word bank: Happy-Sad)
- p.8 The sound of letter Tt — Listen and repeat: toys, turtle, train
- p.9 Let's trace letter Tt (+ trace t inside train, toys)
- p.10 Count and Say: number **1** — "I can see 1 train / 1 turtle"
  (the T words come back as things to count)
- p.11 Trace number 1; color 1 balloon
- p.12 Colors and shapes: "I can see a red circle" — color the circle
  red (first color = red, first shape = circle)
- p.13 The sound of letter Ii — insects, ink, in
- p.14 Let's trace letter Ii (+ i inside ink, in)
- p.15 It's Practice Time! Circle the beginning letter (Tt or Ii:
  turtle, insects)
- p.16 Color page: boy and girl, both saying "Hello."
- p.17 **Story: "Ibrahim and Talia's Day!"** — 4 panels: Hello /
  Good morning → "Do you want to play?" "Yes, we are friends." →
  "I dropped my toy. I am sad." "It's okay. I will help you." →
  Goodbye / Goodbye. (Names are the book's own — source tags only.)

### KG1 Unit 2 — page by page (book pp.18–36; read 2026-10-25)
- p.18 Unit opener: "School" (Look, listen, and point)
- p.19 "Classroom" — labeled picture: board, desk, book, pencil,
  eraser
- p.20 "playground" (picture page)
- p.21 "What can you see at the playground?" — swing, slide (Look and
  say; listen and circle)
- p.22 "What can you see in your classroom?" — Look and say: teacher,
  board, desk, chair, eraser, pencil, book, crayon
- p.23 The sound of Ss — star, sun, school · p.24 Trace Ss (s in sun,
  star)
- p.25 Count and Say: **2** — "I can see 2 chairs / 2 pencils"
  p.26 Trace number 2; color 2 balloons
- p.27 Colors and shapes: blue square ("I can see a blue square")
- p.28 The sound of Aa — apple, ant · p.29 Trace Aa (a in ant, apple)
- p.30 The sound of Nn — nest, night, numbers · p.31 Trace Nn (n in
  nest, night)
- p.32 Practice Time! Circle the picture that matches the letter
  (Ss/Aa/Nn)
- p.33 Practice Time! Color the classroom objects (pencil, chair —
  the plane is not one)
- p.34 **Story: "Look Around the Classroom!"** — "Good morning,
  class. Today we will learn about classroom objects." / "I will
  color the flower with my crayon." / "Hello, Selim. I have a new
  pencil." / "Don't forget to use your eraser if you make a mistake."
- p.35 Project: School Bag (materials page) · p.36 Show and Tell:
  "This is my school bag."

The unit pattern is now confirmed for the whole book: words first
(say them), one letter's sound + trace, a number (count + trace),
a color+shape, the next letters, practice, a 4-panel story, a project,
show and tell.

### KG1 Unit 3 — rest of the unit (book pp.49–62; read 2026-10-25)
- p.49 The sound of Hh — helicopter, honey, heart · p.50 Trace Hh
- p.51 Count and Say: **3** — "I can see 3 pans / 3 helicopters"
  (P and H words counted) · p.52 Trace number 3; color 3 balloons
- p.53 Colors and shapes: yellow star
- p.54 The sound of Dd — dog, desk, dress · p.55 Trace Dd
- p.56 The sound of Rr — robot, rice, rabbit · p.57 Trace Rr
- p.58 Practice: match picture to beginning letter (Pp/Rr/Dd/Hh)
- p.59 Practice: circle the body parts
- p.60 **Story: "All About Me and My Day!"** — "Good morning, Hana!
  I comb my hair." / "I nod my head." / "This is my face." / "I see
  2 eyes, 2 ears, 1 nose, and a mouth." / "I lift my arms." / "I jump
  with my legs." / "I see 1 rabbit and 2 dogs." / **"That makes
  3 animals."** — the whole unit (body, hygiene, counting) inside one
  story; even the adding (1 + 2 = 3) is said in words.
- p.61 Project: My Face (face-part puppets on sticks) · p.62 Show
  and Tell: "This is my face."

### KG1 Unit 4 — page by page (book pp.63–88; read 2026-10-25)
- p.63 Unit opener: Picnic — "We are having so much fun." / "What a

- p.64 My Family (Look, listen, and point): father, mother, brother,

- p.84 Practice: match picture to beginning letter (Ee/Cc/Kk/Mm)
- p.85 Practice: match the picture with its shadow

### KG1 Review — (book pp.89–94; read 2026-10-25)
- p.89 Practice: trace the letters (Tt, Hh, Ss, Kk with their words)
- p.90 Review: match picture with the correct letter, then trace
  (Ee/Ii/Rr/Mm/Aa)
- p.91 Review: circle the good hygiene action; circle the healthy

- p.94 Final Project: "My Classroom Craft!" — color, cut and glue

- Then one blank page and the back cover: English KG-1 Term 1,
  Academic Year **2025/2026**, Ministry of Education and Technical

## ✅ EGYPT KG1 TERM 1 — READ COMPLETE, 2026-10-25
Every page read (cover, both contents pages, book pp.1–94, back
cover). Basics filed into lessons: Pencil and Lines (p.1 grip +
lines); Greetings sources (U1 spoken layer + stories); the-alphabet
sources (all 13 letters' sound/trace pages). Word sets (classroom,
body, family, food, garden), the Sentence patterns ("I comb my
hair", "I can see…", "This is my…", "I love my family") and the four
unit stories are recorded above with their pages — their titles
(Word, Sentence, Story) build from these records in ladder order.
The Math column (numbers 1–5, shapes, colors) is recorded here for
the Maths course.

### KG1 Unit 3 — page by page (book pp.37–48; read 2026-10-25)
- p.37 Unit opener: washing at the sink, mirror (My Body, My Care!)
- p.38 My body parts: face, arm, hand, foot (labeled picture)
- p.39 My face parts: hair, ear, eye, nose, mouth
- p.40 Point to it: eye / ears · p.41: nose, teeth, mouth
- p.42 "How Many? Match the Body Parts" — eyes, feet, ears go with
  **2**; nose, mouth go with **1** (the counting hides inside the body)
- pp.43–45 **Hygiene Actions — the book's first full action
  sentences:** "I comb my hair." · "I wash my face." · "I wash my
  hands." · "I sneeze and cover my nose." · "I brush my teeth."
  (Sentence title: the "I + action + my + body part" pattern starts
  here, in KG.)
- p.46 Color the correct action (right/wrong hygiene judgment page)
- p.47 The sound of Pp — pen, pan, pants · p.48 Trace Pp

Reading continues page by page until the book is finished (one book
at a time — his rule). Filed so far from KG1: Pencil and Lines
(grip + lines, p.1); Greetings (spoken greetings pp.2/4/5 + story
p.17 now in its sources); the-alphabet sources (Tt pp.8–9, Ii
pp.13–14, practice p.15; Ss/Aa/Nn pages recorded here for the same
lesson). Number/shape/color pages are Egypt's own Math column inside
this book — recorded here; they file into the Maths course when it
starts, not into English.

## KG2 Term 1 — reading in progress (started 2026-10-25)

Book: English_KG2_Tr1.pdf — 12,389,586 B, 124 pp, image-only.
Book page = PDF page − 4, same as KG1.

### KG2 full contents (PDF pp.3–4, read)
- Unit 1 "Little Bodies, Big Moves!" — action words (walk, run,
  jump, skip, kick, play, throw, catch) · Structure: "I can jump /
  I can run / I play with my friends / I can kick the ball / I can
  throw the ball / I can catch the ball" · Phonics: **Jj, Kk** ·
  Numbers 1, 2
- Unit 2 "Let's Get Dressed!" — clothes words · Structure: "I have a
  yellow T-shirt" / "My dress is red" · Phonics: **Dd, Mm, Gg** ·
  Numbers 3, 4, 5
- Unit 3 "My Week!" — days of the week, today/tomorrow/yesterday ·
  Structure: "What day is it?" / "Tomorrow is Sunday" / "I go to the
  park on Friday" / "Today is Monday" · Phonics: **Oo, Bb, Ff, Ll** ·
  Numbers 6, 7, 8
- Unit 4 "My Busy Day!" — morning/noon/night, meals, daily routine ·
  Structure: get up, go to bed, go to school, have breakfast/lunch/
  dinner · Phonics: **Ww, Cc, Rr, Uu** · Numbers 9, 10
- Review letters: **Jj Kk Dd Mm Gg Oo Bb Ff Ll Ww Cc Rr Uu** (13) —
  with KG1's 13, Egypt's KG pair covers all 26 letters; numbers 1–10.

### KG2 opens with "Remember With Me!" — a recall section (book pp.1–6)
- p.1 Trace the lines (wavy, zigzag — from a dot) + trace the shapes
  (square, circle, triangle, heart) — the Pencil and Lines material,
  second book
- p.2 **Greetings** — "Listen, repeat, and act out": Hello / Good
  morning · "What is your name?" "My name is Nour." · I'm a boy /
  I'm a girl · We are friends · Goodbye
- p.3 My School, My Classroom — point, say, color: school; classroom
  (board, desk, teacher, chair)
- p.4 My School Bag! — match what belongs in the bag (crayons,
  eraser, pencil, books — not the dog, not the tree)
- p.5 My Family — "Who's this? This is my sister / father / mother /
  grandfather" (listen, point, circle)
- p.6 My Body Parts — "I have 1 head. I have 2 eyes. I have 1 mouth.
  I have 2 arms. I have 2 hands. These are my feet."

- p.7 Opener: "I can kick the ball. I can jump. I can throw the

## ✅ EGYPT KG2 TERM 1 — READ COMPLETE, 2026-10-25
Every page read (contents, book pp.1–118, credits, back cover).
KG2's 13 letters (Jj Kk Dd Mm Gg Oo Bb Ff Ll Ww Cc Rr Uu) join KG1's
13 — Egypt's KG pair teaches all 26 letters, each letter with its
sound words, formation arrows, trace and practice pages, all
recorded above. Filed into lessons: Greetings sources (p.2 recall
page), Pencil and Lines sources (p.1 lines/shapes refresh),
the-alphabet sources (KG2 letter rows). The rest — action verbs and
"I can…", clothes, days, routine, the four medals, songs, stories
and games — is recorded here with pages for its titles in ladder
order.

- p.1 Cover: "English — Primary 2 — Term 1," two children reading,
  Ministry of Education and Technical Education seal.
- p.2 Dedication page ("…with love, to the children and families of
  the Arab Republic of Egypt"), © 2025 Ministry of Education and
  Technical Education, New Administrative Capital, Cairo; Name/Class/
  School box.
- p.3 **A Message from the Ministry** — presents the *Primary 2
  Framework for Egyptian Learners*: the book is organized into weekly
  sessions across Listening and Speaking / Reading and Phonics /
  Writing and Vocabulary / Cultural Integration, with regular
  assessment and feedback.
- p.4 **Scope and Sequence** — the whole term on one page:
  - **Unit 1, Let's Get Started!** — Vocab: hello, goodbye, teacher,
    chair, desk, eraser, book, ruler, bag, window, door, pencil case.
    Language: "Hello, I'm Ali." "What's your name?" "This is a chair."
    "That is a desk." Phonics: short /æ/ cat, hat, cap · short /e/ pen,
    ten, red · short /ɪ/ zip, big, pin. Values: self-introduction,
    respect for teachers, classroom routines.
  - **Unit 2, Colors, Shapes, and Numbers** — Vocab: red, blue, green,
    yellow, circle, square, triangle, 1–20 and numbers in 10s.
    Language: "This is a red circle." "What color is this?" "How many
    pencils do you have?" Phonics: short /ɑː/ dog, box, fox · short
    /ʌ/ sun, cup, bun · long /eɪ/ cake, lake, snake. Values: numerical
    literacy, taking turns and sharing.
  - **Unit 3, Classroom Actions & Routines** — Vocab: write, draw,
    read, color, clean, play, sit, stand, raise, open, close, sing.
    Language: "I read a book." "What do you do?" "Stand up." "Open
    your book." Phonics: initial consonant sounds (sit, clap, draw).
    Values: following instructions, responsibility.
  - **Unit 4, Everyday Life** — Vocab: mother, father, sister, brother,
    grandmother, grandfather. Language: "Who's this?" "This is my
    sister." "This is your brother." Phonics: long /oʊ/ nose, rose ·
    short /ʌ/ sun, cup, bun · digraph /θ/ thin, thumb, teeth, thick,
    thunder, math. Values: family appreciation, kindness.
  - **Unit 5, My Home** — Vocab: table, chair, bed, door, window, lamp,
    cup, plate, cook, clean, help, watch, eat, play, sleep, brush,
    read, sit. Language: "The lamp is on the table." "I help my mother
    clean." Phonics: digraph /ʃ/ ship, shop, fish · digraph /ð/
    mother, father, that. Values: responsibility at home, helping
    others.
  - **Unit 6, Rooms at Home** — Vocab: kitchen, living room, bedroom,
    bathroom, dining room, garden, hall, balcony. Language: "There is
    a kitchen in my house." "There is a bed in my room." Phonics:
    digraph /tʃ/ chair, chin, lunch · long /uː/ glue, cube. Values:
    respecting space, hygiene.

  (Speaking: basic greetings, name 5–7 classroom objects, ask/answer

- p.50 **Unit 4, Lesson 1 — My Family.** Vocabulary with a labeled
  family picture: father, grandfather, sister, mother, brother,

- p.51 Family Tree Game: "Who is he/she?" ("This is my father. He is
  tall.") · Practice: describe your own family with "This is my…"
  and simple descriptions · **Language in Use — my / your / 's**:
  "my" = belongs to the person speaking ("This is my brother.");
  "your" = belongs to the person you are talking to ("This is your
  bag." "This is your toy. These are your toys."); a name takes ('s)
  — "Ahmed has a car. It's Ahmed's car." · Q: "Who's this?" A: "This
  is my father. / This is your mother."
- p.52 Lesson 1 practice pages: Ex.1 Listen, repeat then write
  ("This is my mom." model; "This is my ______." / "This is your
  ______." ×2 against photos) · Ex.2 Ask and Answer — "Who is this?"
  against three photos · Ex.3 Label pictures of family members with
  the correct words (family scene, four labels).
- p.53 **Unit 4, Lesson 2 — All About Me.** Ex.1 Read and match (the
  my-set: sister, brother, grandmother / the your-set: mother,
  sister, pencil case) · Ex.2 Read and complete (sister, bed,
  grandfather in the box; model "This is my mother." → "This is my
  grandfather." / "This is your sister.") · Ex.3 Choose ("This is your
  brother." / "This is your grandmother.") · Ex.4 Reorder ("This is
  my father." / "This is your father.").
- p.54 Ex.5 Reorder to make a word (h/m/r/e/o/t → mother; s/r/e/t/s/i
  → sister) · Ex.6 Write two sentences about your family (model:
  "This is my grandmother. She is kind.") · Ex.7 Trace the words
  (mother, father, sister, brother, grandmother, grandfather).
- p.55 **Phonics Focus: long "o" and "u" sounds** — Ex.1 Listen and
  repeat: nose, rose (long o); glue, cube (long u) · Ex.2 Match the

  (red/rose, nose/net, cub/cube, glue/game) · Ex.4 Write the missing
  letter (r_se, n_se, c_be, gl_e).
- p.56 Ex.5 Rearrange the letters (sero→rose, sone→nose, becu→cube,
  gelu→glue) · Ex.6 Trace (rose, nose, cube, glue) · Ex.7 Draw and
  label a family tree (grandfather – grandmother – father – mother –
  brother – sister).
- p.57 **Unit 4, Lesson 3 — My life at Home.** Vocabulary with real
  photos: wake up, eat, play, sleep, brush, read, wash, walk ·
  "What do you do in the morning?" "I brush my teeth." "I play."
- p.58 **Language in Use — The Present Simple**: "I wake up at 7:00
  am." "He eats at breakfast." Time Expressions: In the morning /
  At night / After school · Ex.2 Write each sentence under the

  seven." / "I play after school." / "He eats breakfast.").
- p.59 Ex.3 Choose the correct answer ("I brush my teeth." / "She

  Game (chain game: teacher begins "I wake up." → add "I wake up,

## PRIMARY 2 TERM 1 FULLY READ — 2026-10-25 (all 104 pages)

Structure, same as the ministry pattern: 6 units × 4 lessons each,
a Review after Unit 3 (pp.44–48) and after Unit 6 (pp.93–101), and a
Glossary (pp.102–103). Unit spans: U1 Let's Get Started pp.5–16 ·
U2 Colors, Shapes, and Numbers pp.17–30 · U3 Classroom Actions &
Routines pp.31–43 · U4 Everyday Life pp.49–63 · U5 My Home pp.64–81 ·
U6 Rooms at Home pp.82–92.

**Every named rule the book states, in its own boxes:** Singular and
Plural — add -s (p.26) · my / your / name+'s (p.51) · The Present
Simple + time expressions (p.58) · Prepositions of Place — on, under,
next to, in (p.66) · There is / There are (p.88) · "a full sentence"
as the Unit 1–3 review's language focus (p.44).

**Phonics spine of the term:** short a (p.8) · short e + short i
(p.13) · short o + short u (p.21) · long a (p.28) · initial consonant
sounds (pp.37–38) · long o + long u (p.55) · digraph th voiceless —
thin, thick, thumb, thunder, math, teeth (pp.61–62) · digraph th
voiced — mother, father, brother, this, that (pp.72–73) · digraph sh
(pp.79–80) · digraph ch — cheese, chicken, chin, lunch, beach (p.89).

**Where its basics file (lessons):** greetings set re-taught higher
(+ "May I sit down?", teacher address) → Greetings lessons' sources,
pp.6–7 · family introductions "This is my father" + "Who's this?" →
introducing-a-friend sources, pp.50–54 · short/long vowel sets →
word-families sources · th/sh/ch sets → two-letter-sounds sources ·
initial sounds → blending-sounds sources · colors/shapes, numbers
1–10 + tens, classroom objects, action verbs, family, home/rooms,
prepositions, present simple, there is/are → Word and Sentence title
material, recorded here and slotted in CURRICULUM.md; their lessons
are built as the Word/Sentence titles' turn comes.

## Primary 2 Term 2 — FULLY READ 2026-10-25 (all 112 pages)

Book facts (verified on disk 2026-10-25): `English_Prim2_Tr2.pdf` —
26,910,676 B, **112 pages**, WITH a usable text layer (read from the
text; cover is the only text-thin page), page rule: printed folio =
PDF page (folio 6 on PDF p.6, folio 78 on p.78). Source: the
library's secured copy on the LiqLearns textbooks Telegram channel
(channel file `English_Prim2_Tr2.pdf`, 25.7 MB, caption "Egypt —
Grade 2 — English Language — English Language Primary 2 Term 2.
Source: Egypt MoE"); working copy in `egypt-english/`, extracted text
in `egypt-english/p2t2-text.txt`.

Identity: cover "2 Primary — Term 2 — 2025/2026" (PDF p.1); © 2025
Ministry of Education and Technical Education, Egypt. The ministry
message (p.3) presents the same Primary 2 Framework: weekly sessions
across Listening/Speaking, Reading/Phonics, Writing/Vocabulary,
Cultural Integration — this term's message adds that each unit
features fables inspired by Middle Eastern folklore. Back page
(p.112): print spec 112 pages (matches the PDF, unlike Term 1's
108-vs-104), 27×19 cm, 70 gsm text / 180 gsm cover, 4 colors;
developed with Selah El Telmeez Publishing and Printing Company;
reviewed by Prof. Zeinab El-Naggar, Prof. Attia El-Sayed, Prof.
Nagwa Younis, Dr. Mai Kamal, Ms. Amira Awaad; Central Administration
for Curriculum Development; General Supervision Dr. Akram Hassan
Mohammed. No ISBN appears in this copy's text layer.

Structure: Scope and Sequence table (p.4); each unit opens with a
Learning Outcomes page and closes with a "What I Learned"
self-assessment. 6 units × 4 lessons, a Review after Unit 3
(pp.55–58), a final Review (pp.106–108), and a Glossary
(pp.109–111). Unit spans: U1 Our Environment pp.5–20 · U2 At the
Store pp.21–36 · U3 Sports pp.37–54 · U4 Transportation pp.59–76 ·
U5 The Pyramids pp.77–94 · U6 A Day with My Family pp.95–105 (a
STORY unit — one story with story-element work, not 4 lessons).

- **U1 Our Environment** — park words: flower, park, grass, tree,
  river, plant, bench, pick, throw, beautiful, bee, buzzing, nice,
  clean, rubbish, bin, grow. Language in Use box (p.13): **There is /
  There isn't** for one thing + "Is there…? Yes, there is / No, there
  isn't." Phonics: long a + long i — game, gate, cake, line, kite,
  five (with a song, p.11). Lesson 4 "Keep it Clean": rubbish in the
  bin, do not pick flowers.
- **U2 At the Store** — stationery shop words: pencil case, pens,
  bag, scissors, ruler, books, coloring pencils; a shop dialog
  ("Are there any pencil cases, please?" — "That will be seventy
  pounds"); Lesson 4 clothes: T-shirt, skirt, shoes, jacket, pants,
  shelf, try on, "great choice." Language in Use box (p.29):
  **There are / There aren't** with plural nouns + "Remember: Add -s
  for plurals." Phonics: long u + long o — nose, cube, tube, rope,
  stone (song p.28).
- **U3 Sports** — football, basketball, tennis, volleyball,
  swimming, running, racket, net, kick, hit, goal, court; actions:
  hit into (the goal / the net), hit over (the net), run around (the
  courts). Phonics: **final blends -mp, -ft, -lt** — belt, salt,
  lamp, jump, gift, left. Counting 20–50 (pp.45–46). Plural -s with
  the book's own pronunciation tip (p.47): plural ends /s/ after
  p, t, k, f sounds; /z/ after b, d, g, l, m, n, r sounds.
- **U4 Transportation** — car, taxi, bicycle, plane, motorcycle,
  walk, drive, ride + city places: library, club, post office, city,
  supermarket, street, letters. Lesson 2 is a phonics REVIEW (all
  four long vowels + final blends -lt/-mp/-ft sorted together,
  pp.65–67). Lesson 3 Language in Use (p.68): **Nouns — person,
  place, thing**, with the "Life in the City" text (Karim) and
  sorting work. Lesson 4 "Fun City Trip" dialog: Nile River,
  amusement park, rides, shopping mall, circus, clowns.
- **U5 The Pyramids** — The Pyramids, Sphinx, desert, school bus,
  camel, guide, tourists, juice, take pictures, camera, trip, huge,
  ride, statue, head, lion, body. Language in Use box (p.86):
  **Present Simple** for habits — "I take pictures. My friend rides
  the camel." — with the rule in words: for he, she, or it, we add
  -s to the verb. Counting 50–100 and counting by 10s (pp.89–90).
  Phonics: **final blends -nd, -sk** — hand, sand, wind, desk, mask,
  flask. Lesson 4 is a reading text, "The Sphinx" (p.91): a very old
  statue near the Pyramids in Giza, body of a lion, head of a man;
  "The Sphinx tells us about old Egypt."
- **U6 A Day with My Family** — the term's STORY: Nour's family
  spends Friday at the park (mother packs sandwiches, fruit, juice;
  bus ride; blue sky, warm weather; football with father; mother
  reads a story under a tree; counting flowers). Words: park, bus,
  sky, weather, grass, flowers, tree, football, pack. The story work
  names its parts: **Characters** (who), **Setting** (where),
  sequence of events, and a printed **Moral**: "The importance of
  spending time with family" / "Family time makes us happy and
  strong" (pp.97–98).

Reviews: after U3 (pp.55–58) — park/store/sports vocabulary, the
long-vowel and final-blend sets re-sorted, There is/are choice work,
plural -s, missing numbers. Final Review (pp.106–108) — phonics
skills (missing letters + rearranged words for the term's sets),
counting by 10s, circle the verb, circle the noun, sort nouns into
person/place/thing, sort nouns vs verbs.

Glossary (pp.109–111): Word / Definition table, alphabetical, one
plain definition per word, ~90 words (Amusement Park "a place with
games and rides" … Weather "how the air is outside"). Like Term 1's
glossary it defines some words the units only touch in passing
(goal, healthy food, letters, photo, student).

The book's own slips, recorded as printed: U3 Lesson 1's pair-work
script (p.39) opens "Hana: Hi, Ali!" but Habiba answers — a speaker
mislabel carried from the main dialog. U4's phonics lesson (pp.65–66)
prints its exercise block twice with small variations (the missing-
letter, match, and circle sets appear two times). The Scope and
Sequence table (p.4) is ragged for U4 (its Language in Use cell reads
only "nouns: dad - car - bicycle - plane") and for U6 (vocabulary
cell ends "pack - -") — the lessons themselves deliver the full
material; the table is the thin part. U3's self-assessment (p.54)
lists the blends as "(mf, ft, lt)" where the unit teaches -mp.

**PRIMARY 2 TERM 2 FULLY READ — 2026-10-25 (all 112 pages).**

**Every named rule the book states, in its own boxes:** There is /
There isn't, singular (p.13) · There are / There aren't + "Add -s for
plurals" (p.29) · plural pronunciation /s/ vs /z/ (p.47) · Nouns =
person, place, thing (p.68) · Present Simple for habits + he/she/it
add -s (p.86) · story parts named: Characters, Setting, Moral
(pp.97–98).

**Phonics spine of the term:** long a + long i (U1) · long u + long o
(U2) · final blends -mp/-ft/-lt (U3) · all long vowels + final blends
reviewed and sorted together (U4) · final blends -nd/-sk (U5).

**Where its basics file (lessons):** the four long vowels completed
and sorted as sets → word-families sources · the final blends
(-mp/-ft/-lt, -nd/-sk) are END blends — a different thing from the
built Blends lesson's start blends, so they are recorded here for
their own future lesson, not merged into it · stationery/clothes/
city/sports/Pyramids word sets, There is/are both forms, plural -s
with its /s/–/z/ tip, nouns person/place/thing, present simple,
counting 20–50 and 50–100, and the Nour story with its named story
parts → Word and Sentence title material (nouns are Term 2 Unit 1's
first real source; the Nour story is Term 4 Unit 4's first), recorded
here and slotted in CURRICULUM.md.

## Primary 3 Term 1 — read in full 2026-10-25 (all 120 pages, visually)

Book facts (verified on disk 2026-10-25): `English_language_prim3_t1.pdf`
— 25,838,419 bytes, ends %%EOF, 120 pages, IMAGE-ONLY (no text layer
at all) — read visually from rendered pages. Downloaded from the
Telegram library channel (message captioned "Egypt — Grade 3 —
English Language — English Language Primary 3 Term 1. Source: Egypt
MoE"; the channel attachment's own name is
`English_language_prim3_t1.pdf`) via the source link in that message:
https://elearnningcontent.blob.core.windows.net/elearnningcontent/2026_2027/Primary/Primary3/Term1/StudentBook/English_language_prim3_t1.pdf
**Page rule: printed folio = PDF page** (checked on every unit).
Identity: cover "English / Primary 3 / Term 1" (p.1); © 2025 MoE
(p.2); ministry message (p.3) names the "Primary 3 Framework for
Egyptian Learners", organized into weekly sessions. Back page
(p.120): ISBN 978-633-8475-02-4; deposit no. 2026/8934; Arabic print
spec says 124 pages / 2026–2027 printing (the secured PDF has 120);
developed, revised and supervised by Prof. Zeinab El-Naggar, Prof.
Attia El-Sayed, Prof. Nagwa Younis, Dr. Mai Kamal, Ms. Amira Awaad,
Dr. Eman Mahmoud Youssef, Dr. Amira Fawzy Ahmed, Ms. Mai Ibrahim
Zaber, Dr. Ghada Elsayed Mohammed, Dr. Akram Hassan Mohammed, Dr.
Gebriel Anwer Hemeida; in participation with Selah El Telmeez;
British Council + CACD marks.

THE BOOK'S SHAPE: Scope and Sequence (p.4); six units, each opening
with Learning Outcomes and closing with a star-rated Self-Assessment
(Not yet / Sometimes / Always); Review 1 after Unit 3 (pp.63–66);
Review 2 after Unit 6 (pp.114–117); Glossary (pp.118–119, ~85 words
by category with one plain definition each). Lessons per unit vary:
U1 4 · U2 4 · U3 4 · U4 3 · U5 4 · U6 4. Unit spans: U1 Let's Learn
Together! pp.5–26 · U2 My Family and I pp.27–42 · U3 New Adventures
pp.43–62 · U4 Let's Tell Stories! pp.67–81 · U5 Together Is Better
pp.82–98 · U6 Dare to Dream pp.99–113.

THE GRAMMAR SPINE (every Language in Use / Language Focus box, in
the book's own order — the richest rule book so far):
1. **Present Simple Tense for Descriptions** + Verb "To Be" table —
   "We use the present tense to talk about who we are and what
   people are like": I am / You are / He is / She is / It is / We
   are / They are (p.16); Asking Questions note ("We use questions
   to learn about our friends").
2. **Present Simple Tense for Routines** with the FULL spelling
   rules (p.23): add -s with he/she/it · verbs ending ch, sh, x, ss
   or o add -es · consonant + y → change y to i and add -es ·
   vowel + y → add -s. (P2 T2 named only "he/she/it add -s"; this
   book completes the rule.)
3. **Possessive Pronouns my / her / his** (p.30, photo examples).
4. **Past Simple Tense (regular)** (p.60): "We use the past tense
   to talk about something that happened. For most verbs, we just
   add -ed… For verbs ending in -e, we add -d" — jump/jumped,
   climb, walk, play, smile, close, dance, like.
5. **Past Simple Tense (irregular)** (p.71): "Irregular verbs are
   special words! we DO NOT add -ed to make the past tense. Their
   past tense form is different" — go/went, eat/ate, is/was,
   are/were, do/did, see/saw, come/came, have/had; more pairs in
   stories: draw/drew, fall/fell, feel/felt, win/won, leave/left,
   take/took, hurt/hurt.
6. **Punctuation Tips** (p.54, restated p.90): capital letters at the
   beginning of the sentence; periods at the end of sentences;
   question marks at the end of questions; a comma after (Then –
   First – Finally).
7. **Prepositions of Place** (p.55): "They are words that tell you
   where something is found" — on, under, behind, between, in
   front of, in (real photos of cats and dogs with a box). Extends
   P2 T1's on/under/next to/in set with behind, between, in front
   of.
8. **Action Verbs** (p.89): "Action verbs are words that show what
   someone does. They tell us about an action" — helps, solve,
   finds, work, plan, fixes, agree.
9. **Linking words** (Tip, p.70): "Linking words make the story
   clear and easy to follow. First, Then, After that, Finally";
   Note box (p.75): Then = what happens next · But = something
   different · And = add more or join; when/after drilled in U4.
10. **Present Continuous Tense** (p.103): "We use the present
    continuous tense to talk about actions happening now or around
    now" — Verb to Be + verb +ing table (am practicing / is trying
    / are celebrating). It also speaks earlier, untaught, in U5's
    dialog ("What are you doing? I am helping clean the park",
    p.95).

THE PHONICS SPINE (Phonics Focus pages):
- **Digraph "wh"** /w/ (p.12): what, when, where, whale, wheel,
  white (+ why in the fill-ins).
- **Letter "x" = /gz/** (p.35): exam, exit, example, examine, exist
  (+ riddles).
- **Digraph "ph" = /f/** (p.38): phone, photo, elephant, graph,
  alphabet (+ riddles).
- **Double Consonant Endings** (p.49) — the book STATES the rule:
  "If a short word has a short vowel sound, and it ends with (F, L,
  S, or Z), we often write two of those letters": ff cuff off
  staff · ll bell pull tell · ss grass less miss · zz buzz fuzz
  jazz.
- **Digraph "ck" = /k/** (p.53): "The letters 'ck' together make
  one /k/ sound (like in 'cat' or 'kite')" — duck, sock, back,
  neck, truck. ( filed under two-letter-sounds.)
- **Hard "c" = /k/** (p.73): "'c' makes a hard /k/ sound when it is
  followed by (a, o or u) or at the end of a word as 'ck'" — cat,
  came, cold, carry.
- **Soft "c" = /s/** (p.76): "'c' makes the soft /s/ sound when
  followed by (e, i, or y)" — face, nice, city, ice, pencil, cent;
  hard/soft sorting box.
- **Hard "g" = /g/** (p.87): "'g' has a hard sound when followed
  by: (a, o, or u) or at the end of a word" — game, dog, gum,
  green, big, good.
- **Soft "g" = /dʒ/** (p.93): "'g' usually makes a soft sound /dʒ/
  when it is followed by (e, i, or y)" — giraffe, Egypt, page,
  huge, magic.
- **Three-Consonant Clusters** (p.105): "Words that begin with
  three consonants" — spr-, spl-, str-, scr-: spring, spray,
  splash, split, screen, scratch, string, street. (Three-letter
  START blends — filed with the Blends lesson's sources; P2 T2's
  END blends stay separate, as ruled there.)

UNIT 1 — LET'S LEARN TOGETHER! (pp.5–26)
- L1 Greetings and Introductions (pp.6–9): the full greeting set as
  one list (Hello! · Good morning! · How are you? · I am fine,
  thank you. · Nice to meet you. · See you later! · Goodbye!) +
  Introducing Yourself (My name is… · I am… · I am from Egypt. · I
  am …. years old. · I am in Primary 3); pair practice; phrase→
  when-you-say-it matching; the "Meeting a New Friend" dialog
  (Sara & Lina, p.7) running the WHOLE conversation in one flow —
  hello, good morning, how are you both ways, names both ways,
  "How old are you? / I am 8 years old!" (the FULL "years old"
  form — P2's short forms and Libya's short answer beside it),
  nice to meet you both ways, see you later, goodbye; fill-in
  dialog and choose-the-right-response listening (pp.8–9).
  → Conversation lessons' sources (hello-and-your-name,
  how-are-you, good-morning-and-good-afternoon, how-old-are-you).
- L2 Classroom Rules (pp.10–12): classroom phrases — Raise your
  hand · Listen carefully · Sit down · Be kind · Keep your
  classroom clean · Don't shout · May I go to the bathroom? (the
  P2 set, grown: "May I…?" joins "May I sit down?"); act-it-out
  game; behavior self-check → classroom/conversation material.
- L3 Describing People (pp.13–18): looks (tall, short, curly hair,
  straight hair, long hair, short hair, brown eyes) + personality
  (funny, shy, friendly, helpful, polite); describing sentences;
  the To Be box (above); reading text "This is my friend Mona…"
  → adjectives/describing material for the Word title.
- L4 Daily Routines (pp.19–26): routine set (wake up, brush my
  teeth, get dressed, make my bed, brush my hair, have breakfast,
  go to school, do my homework + sleep, play, read, write, eat,
  watch TV); photo sentences with clock times ("I wake up at 7
  a.m. / I go to school at 8 a.m."); Amir routine reading (p.22);
  the full Present Simple routines box (p.23); "What time do you
  wake up?" Q&A (p.25) → routines + telling-time material.

UNIT 2 — MY FAMILY AND I (pp.27–42)
- L1 Meet My Family (pp.28–31): family lineup photo-drawn set —
  father, mother, sister, brother, grandmother, grandfather,
  uncle, aunt, cousin ("This is my family"); the possessives box
  (p.30); "My uncle is a police officer" appears in reordering
  (police officer joins the job words in exercises) → family words
  (P2 set restated whole) + the pronouns lesson's his/her depth.
- L2 Family Traditions and Celebrations (pp.32–34): Egypt's days
  by name and picture — Eid Al-Fitr · Sham El-Nessim (garden all
  day, play, eat salted fish) · Mother's Day · 6th of October (the
  brave Egyptian soldiers) — own-country culture material.
- L3 My Family Helps Me Grow (pp.36–38): family-action sentences
  (My father takes me to school · My mother cooks us food every
  day · My brother helps me do my homework · My grandmother reads
  me a story); Nour reading text.
- L4 Jobs in My Family (pp.39–42): jobs by real photo — farmer,
  doctor, teacher, baker, pilot; job sentences (works as a doctor
  · works at a school · bakes delicious bread · travels around the
  world) → jobs word set.

UNIT 3 — NEW ADVENTURES (pp.43–62)
- L1 Places Around Me (pp.44–48): places by real photo — house,
  library, supermarket, park, train station, museum (the museum
  photos are the Grand Egyptian Museum); "Where do you buy food /
  read books / play / live?"; reading text "Youssef's Busy Day"
  (p.47, past-tense day story).
- L2 Egypt's Landmarks (pp.50–52): Cairo Tower · Alexandria
  Library · The Pyramids of Giza by real photo; reading text
  "Laila's Holiday Adventure" (p.51) → Egypt's own-land material.
- L3 Map It Out (pp.55–57): the Prepositions of Place box (above)
  + story "Samy and the Toy Car" (circle the prepositions;
  checked / stuck / stick bolded as new words).
- L4 A School Trip to the Zoo (pp.58–62): past-tense zoo text ("The
  ticket was 20 pounds"); the regular Past Simple box (p.60).

UNIT 4 — LET'S TELL STORIES! (pp.67–81; only 3 lessons)
- L1 How We Feel (pp.68–72): feelings by real photo — happy, sad,
  excited, angry, shy, tired, bored, scared; "How do you feel? —
  I feel so happy!"; story "Adam and Omar in the Garden" (p.70,
  the linking-words Tip; NOTE the same story is reprinted on p.73
  set in "the park" instead of "the garden" — the book varies its
  own text); the irregular Past Simple box (p.71).
- L2 A Helping Hand (pp.74–77): Sara story (bored → drew → fell →
  felt sad and scared → helped → felt better); Remember box
  (go/went, draw/drew, fall/fell, help/helped); the then/but/and
  Note; soft-c phonics (p.76).
- L3 A Big Win (pp.78–80): Omar football story — nervous, worried,
  proud; scored / won; linking words when/but/after; "My Feelings"
  draw-and-write.

UNIT 5 — TOGETHER IS BETTER (pp.82–98)
- L1 Teamwork & Chores (pp.83–86): chores/team words by photo —
  clean, tidy up the bed, share, care, win, cheer, plant, donate;
  "Having Fun" chores text (Mona and Karim; "Good job, team!").
- L2 Teamwork at School (pp.87–90): bridge-building story; the
  Action Verbs box (p.89); story "We Share, We Care" (problem and
  solution named as story parts to find).
- L3 Teamwork in Sports (pp.91–92): "We win together!" story.
- L4 I Help My Community! (pp.94–98): donate/plant sentences with
  one-line definitions printed in the book (plant: to put a flower
  or tree in the ground · donate: to give things to help people);
  helping dialog (p.95); story "We Help Our Neighborhood!".

UNIT 6 — DARE TO DREAM (pp.99–113)
- L1 My Goal, My Dream (pp.100–103): goal words by photo — goal,
  try, step, plan, practice, celebrate, success, reach (the Scope
  and Sequence lists "achieve"; the lessons teach "reach"); story
  "Samer's Goal" (p.102, present continuous inside a story); the
  Present Continuous box (p.103).
- L2 A Day To Remember (pp.104–107): Laila spring story carrying
  the cluster words (spraying, splash, spins, split, screen); the
  clusters phonics (p.105); Mona bike story (p.106 — present
  continuous + First/Then/Now ordering, "practice brings
  success").
- L3 I Did It! (pp.108–109): Tamer exam story (past tense) +
  story frame (First, I wanted to… · Then, I… · After that, I… ·
  Finally, I felt…).
- L4 Helping Others Succeed (pp.110–112): Yara runner story.

THE BOOK'S OWN SLIPS, recorded as printed: exercise lettering goes
a-b-c-b-e twice (pp.24, 25); "Choose the corret verb" (p.79,
missing an r); the Scope and Sequence's U6 vocabulary says
"achieve" where the lessons teach "reach"; the Adam-and-Omar story
changes garden→park between its two printings (pp.70, 73).

ALSO RECORDED: U1's outcomes promise "describe oneself and
others"; every unit's reading texts are short finished stories in
the past or present tense — the Story title's Egypt shelf keeps
growing (Youssef's Busy Day, Laila's Holiday Adventure, Samy and
the Toy Car, Adam and Omar, A Helping Hand, A Big Win, We Share We
Care, We Win Together, We Help Our Neighborhood, Samer's Goal,
Mona's bike, Tamer, Yara).

Filed from this read: Conversation sources (U1 L1, pp.6–9, incl.
how-old-are-you's full "years old" form); two-letter-sounds sources
(wh p.12 · x = /gz/ p.35 · ph p.38 · double-consonant-ending rule
p.49 · ck p.53 · hard c p.73 · soft c p.76 · hard g p.87 · soft g
p.93); Blends sources (three-consonant clusters, p.105);
introducing-a-friend sources (the family/jobs layer, pp.28–42).
The rest — verb to be, present simple full rules, possessives,
past simple regular + irregular, punctuation, the full preposition
set, action verbs, linking words, present continuous, describing
people, routines + times, places, landmarks, celebrations,
feelings, chores, goal words, and the thirteen stories — is
recorded here and slotted in CURRICULUM.md (this book stocks Term 3
heavily: the Sentence book of rules, and Term 4's story shelf).

**PRIMARY 3 TERM 1 FULLY READ — 2026-10-25.** Egypt progress: 7 of
26 English books read (KG1, KG2, P1 T1+T2, P2 T1+T2, P3 T1). Next:
Primary 3 Term 2.

## Primary 3 Term 2 — read in full 2026-10-25 (all 112 pages, visually)

Book facts (verified on disk 2026-10-25): `English_Prim3_Tr2.pdf`
— 4,670,382 bytes, ends %%EOF, 112 pages, IMAGE-ONLY (no usable
text layer) — read visually from rendered pages. Source: the MoE
e-learning host (the same host the Telegram channel's P3 T1 message
linked to):
https://elearnningcontent.blob.core.windows.net/elearnningcontent/2026/Primary/Primary3/Term2/StudentBook/English_Prim3_Tr2.pdf
(The textbooks GitHub repo also secures this book; the host copy
was fetched directly.) **Page rule: printed folio = PDF page.**
Identity: cover "English / Primary 3 / Term 2 / 2025/2026" (p.1);
© 2025 MoE (p.2); ministry message (p.3) — the Term 2 variant
("Each unit features engaging fables inspired by Middle Eastern
folklore"). Back page (p.112): print spec 112 pages (matches the
PDF); book code 30/3/22/1/24/38; reviewed by Prof. Zeinab
El-Naggar, Prof. Attia El-Sayed, Prof. Nagwa Younis, Dr. Mai Kamal,
Ms. Amira Awaad; CACD curriculum team; general supervision Dr.
Akram Hassan Mohammed; produced with Selah El Telmeez's Content
Department; British Council + CACD marks. No ISBN printed.

THE BOOK'S SHAPE: Scope and Sequence (p.4 — now with a "Life Skill
(s) and Value (s)" column: Safety Awareness, Healthy Choices,
Communication, Digital Literacy/Online Safety, Respect for Nature,
Honesty/Integrity); six units, each opening with Learning Outcomes
and closing with a star Self-Assessment (Always / Sometimes / Not
yet); Review 1 after Unit 3 (pp.59–60); Review 2 after Unit 6
(pp.106–108); Glossary (pp.109–111, ~95 words A–Y with one-line
definitions; the book's reference line: "Webster Dictionary for
kids"). Unit spans: U1 Safety pp.5–20 · U2 Food and Health
pp.21–39 · U3 Heroes Around Us pp.40–58 · U4 Living with Technology
pp.61–79 · U5 Animals & Habitats pp.80–95 · U6 The Honest Choice
pp.96–105 (a STORY unit — no lessons, one whole story). EVERY
reading text in this book carries a printed **Moral**, and the
story word itself is taught: Tip (U1) — "Moral is the lesson
learned from a story."

THE GRAMMAR SPINE (Language in Use boxes, book order):
1. **Imperatives** (p.8): "Imperatives are short instructions used
   to tell someone what to do or not to do… Use the base form of
   the verb for positive instructions. Add don't before the base
   form of the verb for negative commands" + the note that they
   end with an exclamation point "because they are strong
   instructions."
2. **Modals for Rules — must / mustn't** (p.14): "We use 'must'
   and 'mustn't' to talk about rules. They tell us what we must do
   and what we cannot do. Form: 'must'/'mustn't' + base form" —
   must = very important or necessary; mustn't = not allowed.
3. **Punctuation: Exclamation point (!)** (p.19): used "after
   instructions that are very important" and "to show strong
   feelings like surprise, excitement, or warning."
4. **Reflexive Pronouns** (p.23): "We use 'reflexive pronouns'
   when the subject and the object are the same person — For
   example, when you do something yourself" — full table: I
   myself · You yourself/yourselves · He himself · She herself ·
   It itself · We ourselves · They themselves. (The pronouns
   lesson's deepest layer yet; P3 T1 gave my/his/her.)
5. **Countable and Uncountable Nouns** (p.36): countable — "We can
   count them (one, two, three…). Use a / an for one item: an
   apple, a carrot. Use numbers for more: two eggs, three
   sandwiches"; uncountable — "We cannot count them individually.
   Use some for a part of it: some rice, some water" (milk, rice,
   pasta, soup, honey, water). Deepens P2 T2's "Nouns = person,
   place, thing" into the counting split, with a/an/some.
6. **Adverbs of Sequence** (p.43): "Use 'first' and 'then' to show
   order — First: what happens at the beginning · Then: what
   happens next." (T1's linking-words set, named as a kind.)
7. **Coordinating conjunctions and / but / or** (p.64), each with
   the book's own gloss: and "expresses addition" · but "shows
   contrast" · or "gives a choice" (the T1 Note's then/but/and,
   completed and named).
8. **Will & Will not** (p.72): "Form — Will/will not + the base
   verb. Will — use it to say something you will do in the future.
   Won't — use it to say something you will not do in the future"
   + Tip: "When writing will not, we often use the short form
   won't." (The future arrives — Term 3's "going to" slot gains
   its first full book rule.)
9. **Adjectives** (p.83): "Adjectives are words that describe
   nouns (people, places, or things). We use them to describe
   animals and their homes" + Tip (p.86): "Adjectives tell us more
   about nouns" (furry bear, sharp claws, strong legs, fluffy
   feathers).
10. **Can & Cannot (can't)** (p.91): "We use 'can' to talk about
    abilities… We use cannot (can't) to talk about things you are
    not able to do" + Note: cannot's short form is can't.

THE PHONICS SPINE:
- **Consonant Blends /sl/ /st/ /gr/ /tr/** (p.11) — with the
  DEFINITION stated: "A consonant blend is when two or more
  consonants are joined together, and you can still hear each
  sound": sl slide slow slip sleep · st star stop step stone stand
  · gr green grow grass grab · tr tree train trip truck trap.
  → Blends sources.
- **"ea" and "ee"** (p.29): "Both 'ea' and 'ee' make the long /iː/
  sound, like in see" — ea usually in the middle (eat, meat,
  beans); ee in the middle or at the end (cheese, green, sweet,
  tree). → word-families sources (long-e team set).
- **Magic e** (p.48): "Some words have a magic 'e' at the end. It
  doesn't make a sound, but it makes the vowel before it to say
  its name" — mad→made, kit→kite, hop→hope. → word-families
  sources: the CVCe pairs complete the long-vowel families.
- **R-controlled vowels er / ar / or** (p.67): er like in teacher
  (computer, teacher, player, letter) · ar like in car (car, star,
  park, guitar, farmer) · or like in corn (corn, horn, fork, short,
  storm). → word-families sources.
- **Long and short "oo"** (p.83): "The letters 'oo' can be tricky
  because they can sound different. Sometimes they are long, like
  in 'moon', and sometimes short, like in 'book'" — long oo /uː/:
  moon, zoo, pool, food · short oo /ʊ/: book, foot, look, cook,
  wood, hook. → word-families sources.
- **Long and short "ow"** (p.90): long ow /oʊ/: snow, grow,
  window, throw, know · short ow /aʊ/: cow, now, owl, how, brown.
  → word-families sources.

UNIT 1 — SAFETY (pp.5–20)
- L1 Safety at Home (pp.6–9): dangerous, rules, emergency,
  careful, hurt, safe (the emergency phone picture shows **122** —
  Egypt's number); Mother–Ali dialog; the Imperatives box; reading
  text with Moral + the "Moral is the lesson learned from a story"
  Tip.
- L2 Safety in the Street (pp.10–12): street, cross, stop, wait,
  walk, helmet, signs, stranger; Mona–Dad dialog; the blends
  phonics; "Crossing the Street Safely" (Moral).
- L3 Staying Safe Everywhere (pp.13–16): policeman, seat belt,
  ambulance, first aid; the must/mustn't box; story "Help is Here!"
  (Lina and the kitchen smoke; Moral); pair work names 122 again.
- L4 My Safety Guide (pp.17–20): story "Let's Stay Safe" (Tia and
  Sally; **nodded** bolded as a new word); the exclamation-point
  box; Safety Guide Poster project.

UNIT 2 — FOOD AND HEALTH (pp.21–39)
- L1 Food for Energy (pp.22–25): food facts text (energy, protein,
  vitamins, sugar, water); the Reflexive Pronouns box; Seleem
  text (reflexives at work; Moral).
- L2 Healthy and Unhealthy Food (pp.26–30): healthy set with
  nutrient lines (milk–bones, cheese–calcium, rice–energy, nuts–
  fats, eggs–protein, bread–carbohydrates, chicken, fish–brains)
  and unhealthy set (sweets, soda, chips, fries, cookies, cake,
  pizza, lollipops); Maya–Salma dialog ("What do you usually eat
  for breakfast?"); the ea/ee phonics.
- L3 Staying Healthy (pp.31–33): exercise, wash, clean, stay,
  rest, medicine; "Maya Learns to Take Care of Herself" (Moral).
- L4 Our Healthy Plan (pp.34–39): yogurt, honey, soup, breakfast,
  lunch, meal, dinner, snack, menu; the Countable/Uncountable box;
  "Omar's Healthy Menu" (Moral); Healthy Day Menu project.

UNIT 3 — HEROES AROUND US (pp.40–58)
- L1 My Hero (pp.41–45): hero words WITH printed definitions —
  hero (someone who does something special, good, or brave) ·
  brave (shows courage in hard times) · strong (can do difficult
  things easily) · kind (being nice and caring about others) ·
  smart (solves problems well); swimmer dialog; the Adverbs of
  Sequence box; "The Brave Firefighter" (Khaled; Moral).
- L2 Heroes in Our Daily Life (pp.46–49): helpful, rescue,
  uniform, teaches; Doctor Ali dialog; the Magic e phonics;
  "Officer Asmaa — The Helpful Policewoman" (Cairo; Moral).
- L3 Hero Stories (pp.50–54): support, inspire (the book names Mo
  Salah: "Mo Salah inspires me to play football"), leader,
  courage, honest (+ printed meanings p.51); flood-girl dialog
  told in First/Then; "A Helping Hero" (Heba; Moral).
- L4 Celebrating Our Heroes (pp.55–58): athlete, respect, event,
  medal; "The Gold Medal" — **Feryal Ashraf**, a real Egyptian
  athlete, named with her gold medal (Moral: hard work brings
  success).

UNIT 4 — LIVING WITH TECHNOLOGY (pp.61–79)
- L1 What is Technology? (pp.62–65): computer, tablet, phone,
  robot, TV; Ali–Omar dialog ("Robots can help people, but they
  can't think like humans"); the and/but/or box; "Samy's Smart
  Start" (Moral).
- L2 Technology in Our Daily Life (pp.66–69): search, camera,
  apps, games, emails (apps defined in a T/F item: "small
  programs for phones or tablets"); the er/ar/or phonics; "The
  Busy Player" (Moral: use technology in a balanced way).
- L3 Using Technology Safely (pp.70–74): screen, button,
  keyboard, password, virus, harm; Amina–Marwa dialog (strong
  password = numbers and letters; never share with strangers);
  printed definitions (virus — "harmful program that can damage
  your computer"; password — "letters and numbers you type to
  unlock something"); the Will/Won't box; "The Secret Password"
  (Moral: keep your information private).
- L4 Technology in the Future (pp.75–79): drone, smart home,
  robot, internet; Hassan–Ahmed dialog; "The Drone Delivery" (set
  in 2050; Moral: technology is fast, but family is the best).

UNIT 5 — ANIMALS & HABITATS (pp.80–95)
- L1 Discovering Habitats (pp.81–84): habitat, ocean, wild,
  desert, forest — defined inside the teacher dialog ("A habitat
  is where animals live"; "Wild means animals that live in nature
  and not owned by people"; "The ocean is big and salty"); the
  oo phonics; the Adjectives box; fable "The Curious Fox" (Moral).
- L2 Describing Animals (pp.85–88): fur, feathers, hoof, nest,
  paw, claws, tail; body-parts text; the adjectives Tip; the
  guess-the-animal game; "The Squirrel's Home" (Squeaky; bushy,
  branches, cozy, gathered bolded).
- L3 What Can Animals Do? (pp.89–92): shelter, survive, crawl,
  fly, swim — defined in the Noha–Maya dialog ("A shelter is a
  safe place where animals stay"; survive "means to stay alive");
  the ow phonics; the Can/Can't box; fable "The Helpful Owl"
  (Moral).
- L4 Protecting Nature (pp.93–95): nature, protect ("It means to
  keep something safe"); "The Helping Hands" (Moral).

UNIT 6 — THE HONEST CHOICE (pp.96–105) — a whole STORY unit
Outcomes are story skills: retell the sequence · name characters,
setting, events · discuss the moral. Vocabulary taught first as a
table WITH printed meanings and pictures (pp.97–98): backpack (a
bag for carrying books) · nervous (worried, not relaxed) · whisper
(to speak very quietly) · copy (to write the same thing) ·
cheating (doing something dishonest) · truth (saying what is real
and true) · break (a stop for a short rest) · choice (a decision
you make). A printed **Story map** (p.98): Characters — Lina (a
student, 8 years old), Miss Salma (the teacher), Nour (Lina's
classmate, 8 years old); Setting — the school. The story
(pp.99–101): Lina forgot her math homework; Nour whispers that she
can copy hers; Lina shakes her head — "That is not right. I should
tell the truth. Cheating is wrong." — tells Miss Salma, is thanked
and given until tomorrow; at break she admits she was scared, and
Nour resolves to be honest too. Printed Moral (p.101): "Honesty is
always the best choice, even when it is difficult." Follow-up work
(pp.102–105): who-said-this, event ordering, word search,
sentences about honesty. — The Story title's fullest single-book
unit yet: story parts (Characters / Setting / Moral) named again
after P2 T2, now with a printed story map.

THE BOOK'S OWN SLIPS, recorded as printed: the punctuation
exercise on p.78 numbers its items 1, 3, 4 (no item 2); U3's Heba
questions ask "What did Heba help build?" where the text says she
helped build hospitals only in the exercise's own answer options,
not in the story text itself.

Filed from this read: Blends sources (the stated blend definition
+ sl/st/gr/tr, p.11); word-families sources (ea/ee p.29 · magic e
p.48 · R-controlled er/ar/or p.67 · long/short oo p.83 · long/short
ow p.90). The rest — imperatives, must/mustn't, exclamation point,
reflexive pronouns, countable/uncountable + a/an/some, sequence
adverbs, and/but/or as named conjunctions, will/won't, adjectives
as a named kind, can/can't, and the safety / food / heroes /
technology / animals word sets plus sixteen moral-carrying texts
and one whole story unit — is recorded here and slotted in
CURRICULUM.md. Primary 3 is now COMPLETE (both terms).

**PRIMARY 3 TERM 2 FULLY READ — 2026-10-25.** Egypt progress: 8 of
26 English books read (KG1, KG2, P1 T1+T2, P2 T1+T2, P3 T1+T2).
Next: Primary 4 Term 1.
